### Page 1

私募巨头 Apollo 正以 750 亿美元的惊⼈押注重塑 AI 芯⽚融资格局 ——350 亿美元购买⾕歌
TPU 租赁给 Anthropic ， 400 亿美元购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚租赁给 SpaceX 。这⼀规模
远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave 的 270 亿美元，使 Apollo 跻⾝仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌的
全球芯⽚储备第四极。
摘要：
私募巨头 Apollo Global Management 正以前所未有的规模押注 AI 芯⽚市场，其两笔待完成的芯
⽚交易合计⾦额⾼达 750 亿美元，不仅远超最⼤ neocloud CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来累计 270 亿
美元的芯⽚融资规模，更是排名第⼆的竞争对⼿的逾 10 倍。
据 The Information 最新报道，SpaceX 宣布将租⽤价值 400 亿美元的英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，⽽
Apollo 将通过旗下⼦公司购⼊这批芯⽚后再租赁给 SpaceX 。此前， Apollo 已于今年 6 ⽉宣布联

合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，为其购买⾕歌张量处理器（ TPU ）并租赁给 Anthropic 的交易安排了
350 亿美元融资。
⼀旦上述交易完成，在 AI 芯⽚持有规模上，Apollo 将仅次于亚⻢逊、微软、⾕歌等超⼤规模云
服务商，成为全球芯⽚储备最雄厚的⾮超⼤规模玩家，彻底改变 AI 基础设施的竞争格局。 Apollo
在这些交易中通过撮合交易及管理融资基⾦赚取费⽤。
两笔巨型交易奠定芯⽚霸主地位
Apollo 当前的芯⽚布局由两笔核⼼交易构成。
其⼀是今年 6 ⽉宣布的 350 亿美元交易：Apollo 联合 Broadcom 与 Blackstone ，购买⾕歌 TPU 并租
赁给 AI 初创公司 Anthropic 。其⼆是上周随 SpaceX 公告浮出⽔⾯的 400 亿美元交易：Apollo 通过
⼦公司购⼊英伟达 Vera Rubin 芯⽚，再租赁给⻢斯克旗下的 SpaceX 。
两笔交易合计 750 亿美元，使 Apollo 在芯⽚融资领域的体量远超任何⼀家 neocloud 。据 The

Information 汇编的数据， CoreWeave ⾃ 2023 年以来通过芯⽚抵押融资累计筹资 270 亿美元，位
居 neocloud 之⾸；其后依次为 Valor （ 54 亿美元）、 Nscale （ 49 亿美元）、 IREN （ 39 亿美元）、
Lambda （ 15 亿美元）、 Crusoe （ 11 亿美元）、 Nebius （ 7.75 亿美元）及 Sharon AI （ 3.56 亿美
元）。
值得注意的是，上述统计仅涵盖以 GPU 和 TPU 为抵押的融资，不包括各家 neocloud 为运营融资
⽽发⾏的可转换债券及信贷额度。此外，上周另有⼀笔由花旗集团、美国银⾏与 Blackstone 联合
安排、规模达 630 亿美元的交易 —— ⽤于为 Anthropic 采购 Broadcom 芯⽚提供融资 —— 亦未纳⼊
上述⽐较，原因是各⽅未披露这批芯⽚的实际持有⽅。
芯⽚融资模式： SPV 结构的规模化复制
Apollo 所采⽤的芯⽚融资结构，脱胎于 CoreWeave 与 Blackstone 在 2023 年共同开创的特殊⽬的

载体（ SPV ）模式。在这⼀安排下， neocloud 与其⾦融合作⽅共同设⽴ SPV ，由 SPV 发⾏债务
以购买芯⽚，芯⽚终端⽤⼾⽀付的租⾦则⽤于偿还债务。
Apollo 的操作逻辑与此⼀脉相承，但规模更为庞⼤。与许多 neocloud 不同的是， Apollo 并未额外
投⼊资本建设⽤于存放芯⽚的数据中⼼，其商业模式更为聚焦 —— 通过购买芯⽚、出租给终端
⽤⼾，赚取交易撮合费⽤及基⾦管理费。

逻辑推向新的量级。

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications

factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed

make sense, in our view.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

Document 353: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

Document 411: The nowcasts, updated scenario and forecasts of the Economic Research - 2 November 2026 (2026-11-02)

here

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2
nd 
Novem
ber 2026

### Page 2

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.6% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of German defence spending on the real economy . Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we anticipate another 
25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That said, strong er than 
expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q  in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aero nautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before  rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.5% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

聯發科 2454 TT ( 電子上游–IC 設計 ) 
件 ) 
金麟化龍，再登巔峰 
投資與評價 
1. ASIC 貢獻優於預期、以及 smart edge platform 挹注下，抵銷手機下
滑影響，預估 2026 年營收將年增 14.2%。 
2. 預估 ASIC 貢獻將於 2027 更加驚豔，上修營收貢獻至 5000 億台幣水
準，手機則呈現回溫態勢，預估 2027 年營收年增 83.3%。 
3. 預估聯發科 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 73.85/171.17 元，考量 ASIC 成長
性，AI 能見度高且延續性佳，以歷史本益比區間中間偏上位置 28 倍為
評價基礎，目標價 4800 元，維持買進投資評等。 
重點更新 
AI 成長優於預期，抵銷手機疲弱影響，上修 2026 年營收至年增 14.2% 
手機業務在 3Q26 年季減 1%，聯發科轉向高端機種來因應原物料成本上升
問題，在客戶陸續推出搭載聯發科 SoC 的新 AI 旗艦手機下，研究部修正預
估手機整年衰退約 16.2%。smart edge platforms 今年主要受惠於 AI 平板 、
AI PC 及車用座艙等專案量產，預估整年營收年增 13.5%，power IC 業務受
惠資料中心相關業務市占提升，研究部上修整年成長約為 5%。今年 AI ASIC
研究部預估貢獻從 20.8 億美元上修至 25.1 億美元營收，佔整體營收 11.4%
客戶需求優於預期，且獲得供應鏈的支援。整體而言，預估 2026 年營收隨
著在 AI ASIC 以及 smart edge platforms 貢獻下，可抵銷手機下滑影響，並
上修成長幅度，營收預估年增 14.2%，達 6804.5 億元，毛利率因成本上升以
及 ASIC 毛利較低，略為下滑至 46.2%。 
AI ASIC 迎來爆發期，算力需求令人驚艷，上修 2027 營收年增 83.3% 
AI ASIC 強進需求將延續至 2027 年整年，而根據調研下一代 ASIC 產品不管
在規模與定價皆高於第一個專案，推估將於 1Q28 進入量產 ，2027 年來自資
料中心 ASIC 營收上修至貢獻超過 5000 億台幣規模(約多 1200 億元)，營收
佔比將來到四成水準，佔比高於先前約 6%。而隨著 AI 導入生活以及相關應
用需求的增長下，算力及高效連結雲端為必備功能，手機部分 在低基期下預
估將成長約 23%。而在邊緣 AI 部分，隨著代理 AI 趨勢，邊緣設備迎來結構
性增長，預估維持高速成長態勢。研究部預估 2027 年營收隨著在 AI ASIC 爆
發及手機回溫下，營收將年增83.3%，突破一兆大關，並且毛利率雖因 ASIC
占比較高下略有稀釋，但 ASIC 營業利益率較高，整體提升至 23.17%。

資料來源：台灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 2

訪談內容 
需求毫無疑慮，成長順勢而起 
手機方面，AI 落地需求以及作為日常生活必需品，研究部認為代理 AI 創新加速發展，公司正與
Android 生態系合作，推動整合至 SoC，並看好其應用延伸至 wearable、mobile、IoT、PC 與
automotive。

在 smart edge platforms 方面，搭載 N1X 的 NVIDIA RTX Spark AI PC 於 10 月中旬透過主要
PC OEM 夥伴上市，結合 NVIDIA GPU 與聯發科 SoC，支援裝置端代理式 AI。Dimensity 9500
搭載於 Samsung Galaxy T ab S12系列 AI 平板，為 Samsung AI 平板連續第三代採用聯發科旗
艦 SoC。

AI ASIC 方面，第二款 AI 加速器封裝方案執行進展良好，目標較前代提高數倍每瓦效能並降低總
持有成本，部分晶粒已完成 tape-out，較大且複雜的晶粒仍依時程推進，將與美國 IDM 封裝夥
伴及台、日基板供應商合作改善良率，並爭取需求上行所需產能，研究部預估 1Q28 進入量產。

ASIC 亦已取得不同於既有主要客戶的第二家資料中心 ASIC 客戶，專案已啟動，預計2028 年開
始量產及貢獻營收， 另外資料中心業務將從晶片延伸至系統與機櫃 ， 透過NVIDIA NVLink Fusion
將客製化 XPU 接入 NVLink 生態系，並支援 UALink，降低部署複雜度、縮短上市與營收實現時
間，並持續推進高速互連、先進封裝與 CPO，448Gbps SerDes 已完成 tape-out，後續進行量
測、驗證及互通性開發，服務更廣泛客戶。

資料來源：國泰證期研究部預估

### Page 3

聯發科 ESG 政策 
聯發科主要從事 IC 設計 ， 產業類別歸類在半導體行業中 ， 依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB
準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料
管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。由於聯發
科為無廠 IC 設計公司，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於IC 設計公司而言是相當重要的
資產，故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體排放及水和廢水管理等則於後簡述。

1. 員工健康與安全： 
為確保承攬商施工安全管理 ， 對於入場施工之人員均辦理職業安全衛生講習 ， 進行危害告知及宣導安全
施工規範，以預防職業災害發生。2022 年參與安全講習之承攬商共有 793 人並達成年度零災害，零
意外、零損失之目標。

圖 1 員工失能、職業病、缺席比率

資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 員工多元化和包容性： 
聯發科技重視研發創新及國際化營運，視全球人才布局為技術研發的驅動力。截至  2022 年年底，聯
發科技員工總人數為 18,993 人。為有效運籌全球研發能量，聯發科技在全球設有 32 個辦公據點，
海外人員數占總人數約 35.4%。期待透過全球人才交流合作，加速產品開發時程與技術支援即時性。

圖 2 員工年齡架構 
資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

3. 溫室氣體排放： 
聯發科技能資源使用所排放之溫室氣體，排放來源包含外購電力、公用設備、鍋爐、冷卻水塔、冰水主
機等，主要排放源為範疇二的外購電力占整體 93.27%。在公司營運規模持續成長的表現下，致力提高
能源使用效率與規劃再生能源使用，以降低公司營運所產生的溫室氣體排放量。2022 年度主要減量作
為包含持續提升資料中心之能效，及完成既有辦公室據點照明更新為 LED 等，以提高用電效率。

圖 3 2022 年溫室氣體排放情形

資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水和廢水管理： 
聯發科技之辦公室取水來自各區自來水廠供水，因今年度員工人數增加，且新建置 5 個辦公據點以及 
IT 資料中心之擴充 ， 共取用 485,629 公噸 （約 486 百萬公升） 水量 ， 其取水量相較於前一年增加 27%，
也因此無法達成 2021 年所設定之目標 5%，雖總用水量提升，聯發科技持續進行水資源項目改善計
畫，平均節水比例達 3.7%，未來將持續搭配進行各項用水減量措施，達成每年減少用水 5% 的目標。
廢水主要為生活污水 ， 依各區納管統一由廢水處理廠處理後排放至自然水體 ， 不會對當地環境產生影響
與衝擊，2022 年排放 202,792 公噸（約 203 百萬公升）廢水，其廢水量相較於前一年增加12%，與
前述用水量增加之原因相同。

圖 4 用水量、節水量與節水比例圖  
資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

中期选举后，宏观变局与资产传导

### Page 2

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
“蓝色狂潮”是民众对特朗普第二任期的强烈否认
民主党在美国中期选举中大获全胜，夺得两院控制权，在选民普遍不满经济状况和伊朗战争背景下，此结果可谓对特朗普第二任期的直接否定
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 在特朗普以压倒性优势返回白宫两年后，“蓝色狂潮”成为他的中期成绩单。民主党在美国中期选举中大获全胜，夺得国会两院控
制权。民主党在全美各地攻下共和党传统票仓，至少翻转7个本属于共和党参议院席位，并在多个共和党2024年轻松取胜的选区赢
得众议院选举。民主党的压倒性胜利意味着，自特朗普去年重返白宫以来，其在华盛顿的权力首次受到真正制衡。尤其需要注意
的是，一些特朗普明确背书的共和党参议员本次选举出现意外落败，凸显了特朗普本人或成为共和党政治上的拖累。

### Page 3

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
民主党在多个传统红州拿下州长席位
本次36个州换选州长，民主党在多个传统红州拿下州长，而州长对本州内AI数据中心建设等事务具有重要影响力，当前美国数据中心高度集中在近期摇摆州
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 本次36个州换选州长，多个传统红州德克萨斯州、乔治亚州、内华达州、俄亥俄州、爱荷华州州长由蓝转红。
◆ 州长对本州内诸如AI数据中心建设相关政策等事务具有重要影响力，本次中期选举，适逢AI主题权益板块承受逆风，民主党相较共和党尤
其是特朗普政府对AI产业在基建审批、行业监管等方面有着更为严苛政策立场，或倾向于延缓数据中心建设、拉长审批周期、提升开发和
投资成本等。

### Page 5

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
“Affordability Crisis”是特朗普的“政治成本”
美伊战争背景下的高油价和升息周期，带来的不仅仅是金融市场“参数变化”，更是加重了美国普通民众的“可负担性危机”
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 2025年，定义特朗普第二任期第一年的关键词
是混乱的关税战，但全球贸易的强大惯性、产
业链联通程度叠加横空出世的AI产业链强大的增
长引擎仍旧拖住全球经济韧性和资产的表现，
冲淡了特朗普的影响。
◆ 而2026年，持续性超出预期的美伊战争定义了
特朗普第二年，此时AI产业链增速边际放缓、资
本开支可持续性风险浮现，此时地缘-通胀风险
加大了全球经济和资产波动，从降息周期陡然
进入升息周期，带来的不仅仅是金融市场“参数
变化”，更是加重了美国普通民众的“可负担性危
机”。
◆ 在此背景下，中选过后是否有继续扩大美伊战
争的可能性？——地缘虽难以预测，但升级战

争恐继续消耗特朗普和GOP的政治筹码，甚至
继续拖累2028年大选。

### Page 6

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
美国政经变化几何？——三类路径看可为和不可为
参议院多数立法需60票（预算协调除外），且总统可否决，因此民主党掌控一院或两院，主要作用是阻止共和党议程，而非推行自己的议程
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 不依赖新立法、总统可自行决定：如232、301关税已在实际执行，对华301条款10月7日确认继续有效；先进芯片出口许可与对外
投资审查已有法律基础（《综合对外投资国家安全法》）、军事行动属总统权限——无太大变化，国会能做的只有拨款附加条件、
听证和诉讼，属于降低行政执行效率的施压手段。
◆ 需要新立法+参议院60票+总统签署：如企业税率从21%上调至25%—28%、恢复ACA增强补贴与联邦医保补贴、恢复被削减的IRA清
洁能源税惠、收回总统关税授权等——民主党推行自己的议程并非易事，对当前市场主线影响不大。普通法案在参议院需60票才
能结束辩论，推翻否决需两院各三分之二多数

◆ 必须通过的法案与参议院专属权力：如12月11日到期的临时拨款（覆盖全部12项年度拨款）、已在众议院以216:212通过的2027财
年国防授权法、延至12月11日的《国防生产法》授权、2027年债务上限；以及参议院人事确认权（美联储理事、联邦法官、内阁）
与众议院传票、监督权等。

### Page 7

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
共和党锚定败选因素直指战争，地缘再度升级民意压力较大
原油价格整体中枢居高不下，现货端有所回落供给紧缺有所缓和，但远月价
格逐步抬升，中长期溢价并未打破
原油结构看，当前并非定价现货紧缺关键，而是定价更持续的地缘溢价
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 但中期选举后，部分共和党明显将败选原因归因于“战争”，提升后续地缘升级的美国内部政治压力。
◆ 对于原油市场而言，我们的观点是“去繁存简”，中长期仍旧视作宏观风险。过去数周，原油市场核心叙事是“供给已然回归，较战
前缺口减少”，但若从更大的角度上看，去繁存简，当地缘核心矛盾未解决的情况下：1）供给持续回归具有脆弱性（伊朗仍有袭
击海峡邮轮的能力、胡塞武装仍有干扰沙特原油供应能力。2）运费价格居高不下、全球原油库存较低支持原油估值。这使得当前
静态供给回归无法有效体现在油价回落、宏观风险缓和这一逻辑上。

联储加息周期定价尚未结束，但空间亦有限
FOMC按兵不动，前方整体定价本轮加息周期不足两次，较9月底鹰派定价峰
值已有所回落
FED隐含利率加/降息次数显示12月、3月加息后暂停，并在2027年Q3转为降
息定价
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 美联储加息周期定价尚未结束，不过从空间上看亦有限。当前FOMC10月按兵不动，整体看，前方整体定价本轮加息周期不足两次
（50bp），货币政策本周期加息幅度较9月底鹰派定价峰值已有所回落，货币政策收紧预期“峰值已达”。
◆ 从曲线上看，FED隐含利率加/降息次数显示12月、3月加息后暂停，并在2027年Q3转为降息定价。这意味着远期看，联储从升息
转为降息的定价终将发生。关注12月加息落地后，市场是否有提前交易加息尾声的可能性。

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
10月初以来看空美股：核心在对AI基本面定价隐含的非对称风险
资料来源 ：彭博 ，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：彭博，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 我们自10月初以来持续看空美股的底层逻辑：核心在于对AI基本面定价隐含的非对称风险尚未出清，利好阈值抬升而利空阈值降低
◆ 自2025Q4开始，“AI交易”的风险点逐步浮出，我们大致构建了对AI产业风险定位的框架：①一阶段（杀估值）是在AI下游商业化“增速”见
顶放缓后对“AI二阶导”反转的确认（对应6-8月全球科技板块调整的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负
&债务融资信用风险攀升背景下对于远期Capex持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）；③三阶段（杀
业绩）是在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI一阶导”反转的确认（尚未出现，但风险随时间累积）。
25Q4以来海外信用市场率先定价Capex融资持续性与回报约束的风险，而海外

权益市场定价明显隐含非对称风险
6-8月市场基本完成了对于“AI二阶导（增速）”反转的确认，9月以来对于“AI一
阶导（增长）”反转在预期层面的质疑亦有所加深

昨夜（11.5）美股大跌催化：民主党横扫强化AI监管担忧
资料来源 ：公开资料搜集 ，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：公开资料搜集，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 昨夜美股大跌的驱动：美股尚未充分消化AI基本面隐含风险的基本盘下天
然对于利空敏感，本周民主党横扫强化AI监管的担忧持续发酵（左图），
叠加高通等科技巨头财报不及预期进一步强化AI基建周期属性构成昨夜美
股大跌催化。
◆ 对于《ASI Prohibition Act of 2026》短期情绪宣泄成分或大于实质性的影
响：11月4日，作为《2026年禁止人工超级智能法案》联合提案人的安萨
里再次公开呼吁，将暂停先进AI研发、加强行业监管及禁止ASI列为民主党
的优先议程。当前，该法案处于初始立法阶段、尚未通过委员会审议，不
宜过度线性外推，且联邦立法仍受到总统否决权制约。
◆ 地方层面对于数据中心潜在的（电力、环保、拿地）约束可能是更为实质
性的影响：民主党横扫可能会导致更严格的监管、更漫长的审批周期，以

及对电力消耗、环境影响、税收优惠和公用事业成本更严格的审查。所有
这些都会推高数据中心的建设成本。

Special report on Guotai Junan Futures
增长在行业的维度：中长期看，财务压力将持续至2027年
全球主要AI投资方在收入、资本开支和自由现金流趋势，显示财务压力趋势仍旧在
延续，2026Q4至2027年自由现金流进入负值区域，意味着中长期看财务压力将持
续至2027年，在此背景下，AI板块料将延续中长期基本面压力，并且对宏观环境要
求较高（对利率、地缘等敏感度提升），当前，AI融资呈现资本开支延续、负债压
力增大，若持续财务压力若使得负债压力最终从部分公司传导至整体投资方，并兑
现资本开支放缓，则AI风险将会从一阶段下游、二阶段中游向三阶段上游硬件传导。
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究
若2027年下游未大规模兑现商业化场景，AI基建定价重回周期股叙事（先杀估
值，而后面临业绩下修的压力）。历史经验，北美CSP估值下修往往领先盈利下
修周期1-3季度。展望2027年，中性假设下北美AI基建股盈利降速将逐步兑现，

构成我们中期看淡美股的底层逻辑

政治噪音不改基本面逻辑，但可把握节奏统计规律
中期选举年和非选举年月度标普指数平均波动率 中期选举年和非选举年月度标普指数平均涨跌幅
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，BofA，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，BofA，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 从历史规律上看，大选年表现往往逊色于非大选年，市场波动率系统性高于非选举。统计自1928年以来中期选举年标普指数月度
平均涨跌幅显示选举年标普的平均波动率高于非选举年，其中9、10月份波动率显著走高，11月波动率回落。在选举年呈现出较强
的节奏规律——中期选举年呈现出“Q3承压、Q4反弹”的典型格局。

政治噪音不改基本面逻辑，但可把握节奏统计规律
从历史统计上看，党派胜负、白宫与国会是否分裂对股票市场而言皆非主要逻辑，政治不确定性落地后，未来3-12月表现录正收益较大
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，BofA，国泰君安期货研究
资料来源：Bloomberg，BofA，国泰君安期货研究
◆ 最值得关注的或许是中期选举后的市场表现。从历史统计上看，党派胜负、白宫与国会是否分裂对股票市场而言皆非主要逻辑，
政治不确定性落地后，统计规律往往显示大选结束后一个季度时间股票上涨概率较高。 若取二战后1950年以后样本，选举后3个
月、6个月和12个月的正收益概率较高，中期选举结束后12个月平均回报分别达18.3%。

中选后，宏观主线逻辑及大类资产配置策略建议
资料来源 ：Bloomberg，国泰君安期货研究

◆宏观背景和主线逻辑：经济结构分化延续、指数层面和景气度资产风险衡量不一、三大宏观风险尚未本质性出清，整体
配置策略宜更为谨慎，重视均衡配置和强弱对冲。
➢ 全球经济增速相对稳定，但“K型分化”特性延续甚至有所加剧，海外看投资、生产强于消费，国内看外需和生产强于
内需消费，总量韧性背景下，AI产业作为增长中最重要的一环，其边际增速成为市场博弈重点。
➢ 三大风险：1）地缘-原油-通胀风险：中东地缘局势难言本质性缓和，尽管供给有所回归，但仍有其脆弱性，同时
柴油、汽油等成品油价格仍旧偏高，原油低库存现状亦支撑其估值。中长期中选后地缘升级于美国内部压力提升，
赔率上看原油支持高位回落，但后续持续挤出地缘溢价仍旧看中东局势是否实质性改善。
➢ 2）产业增长持续性考验：①一阶段（杀估值）是在AI下游商业化“增速”见顶放缓后对“AI二阶导”反转的确认（对应

6-8月全球科技板块调整的底层逻辑）；②二阶段是在中游CSP投资回报率回落&自由现金流转负&债务融资信用风
险攀升背景下对于远期Capex持续性预期层面质疑的加深（对应9月以来海外股债背离加深的底层逻辑）；③三阶段
（杀业绩）是在上游硬件价格松动、上游营收和盈利“增长”见顶回落后对于“AI一阶导”反转现实端的确认（尚未出现，
但兑现的风险随时间累积增加）。
➢ 3）宏观流动性边际变化，货币政策定价鹰派峰值回落：前方整体定价本轮加息周期不足两次（50bp），货币政策
本周期加息幅度较9月底鹰派定价峰值已有所回落，货币政策收紧预期“峰值已达”。而股债同源，若增长最终存风险，
长债具备配置价值，前期空股多债观点兑现，当前股票+短久期国债+增配长久期国债均衡配置

[台湾]～世界的な AI・半導体関連需要の旺盛さがけん引役となり、９月の輸出受注額は過去最高を更新～ 
20 日に発表された９月の輸出受注額は前年同月比＋62.4％となり、 前月 （同＋71.4％） から伸び
が鈍化した。 しかし、 前月比 （季調済） は＋1.1％と前月 （同＋5.9％） から伸びが鈍化したものの、
拡大基調を維持しており、単月ベースの輸出受注額は過去最高を更新している。品目別では、光学
機器関連の受注に下押し圧力が掛かっているものの、 半導体をはじめとする電子部品関連の受注が
引き続き旺盛な動きをみせているほか、情報通信関連、電気製品関連、化学製品関連などでも拡大
の動きが続いている。国・地域別でも、最大の受注先である米国向けが大きく拡大しているほか、
中国本土・ 香港向けや欧州向けも堅調に推移し、ASEAN（東南アジア諸国連合） 向けなど新興国向け

### Page 2

2/4 
も拡大しており、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛さが輸出受注を押し上げている
様子がうかがえる。 
23 日に発表された９月の失業率（季調済）は3.33％となり、前月（3.32％）から0.01pt 悪化し
た。失業者数は前月比＋0.1 万人と前月（同▲0.1 万人）から２ヵ月ぶりの増加に転じている。既
卒者、新卒者問わず全般的に増加基調に転じているほか、自発的失業、非自発的失業ともに増加す
る動きが確認されている。一方の雇用者数は前月比▲0.4 万人と前月（同＋0.2 万人）から２ヵ月
ぶりの減少に転じており、 世界的なＡＩ・半導体需要の旺盛さを反映して製造業で持ち直しの兆し
がみられるほか、建設業では堅調さがうかがえるものの、サービス業で頭打ち感が出ている。雇用
環境に変化の兆しが出ていることを受けて、 労働力人口は前月比▲0.2 万人と前月 （同＋0.1 万人）
から２ヵ月ぶりの減少に転じており、労働市場には一部で弱含みの動きがうかがえる。

[ニュージーランド]～主力の乳製品や食肉を中心に輸出は堅調も、輸入は頭打ち感が強まる～ 
19 日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋12.3％となり、 前月 （同＋12.4％） からわずかに
伸びが鈍化した。 しかし、 前月比は＋0.6％と前月 （同＋5.2％） から増加ペースが鈍化したものの、
２ヵ月連続で増加するなど持ち直しの動きが続いている。 財別では、 原油価格の調整を反映して原
油の輸出額が大きく下振れしたほか、 機械製品関連や果物の輸出額も下振れしたものの、 主力の輸
出財である乳製品や食肉の輸出が堅調に推移していることが輸出全体を下支えしている。 国・地域
別では、米国向けやＥＵ向け、日本向けなどが軒並み低調となる一方、最大の輸出先である中国向
けやＡＳＥＡＮ諸国向けが堅調に推移しているほか、 隣国オーストラリア向けにも底堅さがうかが
える。一方、輸入額は前年同月比＋17.7％となり、前月（同＋13.2％）から伸びが加速した。ただ
し、前月比は▲0.2％と前月（同▲7.9％）に続いて２ヵ月連続で減少しており、頭打ち感が強まっ
ている。財別では、原油・石油製品関連の輸入が堅調に推移する一方、機械製品関連のほか、プラ
スチック関連などの素材・部材関連の輸入が下振れしたことが輸入全体の重しとなっている。 その
結果、貿易収支は▲19.71 億ＮＺドルと前月（▲15.36 億ＮＺドル）から赤字幅が拡大した。

[マレーシア]～世界的な AI・半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に、９月の輸出入額はともに過去最高に～ 
19 日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋1.8％となり、 前月 （同＋1.9％） からわずか
に伸びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.07％と前月（同＋0.29％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、政府
による電気料金の負担軽減措置などを背景に住居・光熱費関連の物価上昇率が鈍化したほか、 食料
品価格の上昇の動きも一服しており、 生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が後退したことが影響して
いる。 なお、 価格変動の大きい生鮮食品や政府の価格管理対象品目などを除いたコアインフレ率は
前年同月比＋1.7％となり、 前月 （同＋1.7％） から２ヵ月連続で横ばいとなった。 前月比は＋0.29％
と前月（同＋0.29％）と同じペースで上昇しており、基調的な物価上昇圧力は根強く残っている。
一方、 輸送関連の物価上昇率が加速するなど、 エネルギー価格の上昇による影響が一部で顕在化し
ているほか、外食・宿泊関連の物価上昇率も加速しており、物価の動きには品目ごとにばらつきが
生じている。 
同日に発表された９月の輸出額は前年同月比＋54.5％となり、前月（同＋45.5％）から伸びが加
速した。 前月比 （季節調整値） も＋10.0％と前月 （同＋1.3％） から上昇ペースが加速して３ヵ月連
続で拡大しており、 単月ベースの輸出額は過去最高を更新するなど堅調な動きをみせている。 財別
では、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛さを反映して、 半導体など電子部品関連の
輸出が大幅に押し上げられたほか、 原油や天然ガスなどの価格上昇を受けて、 鉱物資源関連の輸出
額も拡大した。一方、パーム油をはじめとする農産品関連の輸出は減少したほか、化学製品関連の
輸出も弱含むなど、 財ごとの動きにはばらつきがみられる。 一方の輸入額は前年同月比＋52.7％と
なり、 前月 （同＋41.1％） から伸びが加速した。 前月比 （季節調整値） も＋11.9％と前月 （同＋1.2％）
から上昇ペースが加速して４ヵ月連続で拡大しており、 単月ベースの輸入額も過去最高を更新した。
輸出の堅調さを反映して、 生産活動に必要な機械製品関連の輸入が大幅に押し上げられたほか、 化
学製品関連などの素材・部材の需要も旺盛に推移している。さらに、原油価格などの上昇を受けて
鉱物資源関連の輸入額も押し上げられている。 その結果、 貿易収支は＋337.24 億リンギとなり、 前
月（＋280.85 億リンギ）から黒字幅が拡大した。

[シンガポール]～生活必需品やサービス物価に上昇圧力は残るも、インフレ率・コアインフレ率は鈍化～ 
23 日に発表された９月の消費者物価は前年同月比＋2.2％となり、 前月 （同＋2.3％） からわずか
に伸びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.21％と前月（同＋0.62％）から上昇ペースが鈍化したものの、生
鮮品をはじめとする食料品価格は上昇しているほか、電気・ガス価格も高水準で推移するなど、生
活必需品では物価上昇圧力が根強く残る動きがみられる。 住居費と民間輸送費を除いたコアインフ
レ率も前年同月比＋2.0％となり、前月（同＋2.2％）から伸びが鈍化した。前月比も＋0.06％と前
月（同＋0.27％）から上昇ペースが鈍化しており、エネルギー価格の高止まりを反映して輸送関連
サービスの価格に押し上げ圧力がかかる一方、 金融市場におけるＳＧドルの名目実効為替レートの
上昇が輸入物価の上昇圧力を和らげる方向に作用していると考えられる。 さらに、 家具や衣料品な
どの小売関連を中心に財価格の上昇圧力が和らいでいるほか、 サービス物価も上昇ペースが鈍化し
ている。一方で、食料品や一部のサービス価格には上昇圧力が根強く残っており、物価の動きにば
らつきが生じている。

Document 695: 日本と英国の米ドル建て名目GDPの逆転を考える

Source PDF: all report pdf/日本と英国の米ドル建て名目GDPの逆転を考える.pdf

### Page 1

2026 年 11 月 6 日

MURC Focus

日本と英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の逆転を考える

～円安が経済成長につながっていないことが問題～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 日英の米ドル建て名目 GDP が 2027 年に逆転 
国際通貨基金（IMF）が今年 10 月に発表した最新の『世界経済見通し』の中で、2027 年にも日本と英国の米
ドル建て名目 GDP（国内総生産）が逆転するとの予測が示された（図表 1）。日本の米ドル建て名目 GDP はコロ
ナショック後の 2022 年に 5 兆ドルの節目を割り込んで以降、低迷が続いている。対照的に、英国の米ドル建て
名目 GDP はコロナショック以降に増加が顕著である。

所得水準の国際比較に用いられる一人当たりの米ドル建て名目GDP（図表 2）も、コロナショック前までは日英
ともにほぼ 4 万ドル程度で拮抗していた。一方、コロナショック以降の英国は一人当たり GDP の増加が目覚まし
いが、反面で日本は低迷が続いている。こうしたトレンドが今後も続くと IMF は予想しているわけだが、その理由
は一体どこにあるのだろうか。

〇IMF が今年10月に発表した最新の『世界経済見通し』の中で、 2027年にも日本と英国の米ドル建て名目
GDP が逆転するとの予測が示された。 
〇一方、コロナショック以降の英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加は高インフレを主因とするものであり、英国
民の所得の増加を反映したものとは言い難い。 
〇経済成長を伴わない英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加も問題だが、一方で日本の米ドル建て名目 GDP
の減少も大きな問題である。日本も、円安に依存するこれまでの考え方を冷静に見つめ直すべきだろう。

### Page 2

2. 物価要因と為替要因に起因する英国の GDP 急増 
2020 年以降の英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の推移を①成長要因（実質 GDP の成長率）と②為替要因（対ド
ルレートの変化率）、③物価要因（GDP デフレーターの変化率）に分解してみたものが図表 3 である。これによる
と、特に 2023 年以降の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加は主に物価要因（高インフレ）で説明でき、それを為替要因
（ポンド高）が増幅している関係にある。

言い換えると、コロナショック以降の英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加は、成長要因（好景気）に伴う現象と
は評価できない。加えて 2025 年以降は、ポンドの実質ベースでの対米ドルレートが名目ベースを上回っており、
ポンドが割高に評価されていると判断される（図表 4）。このポンドの過大評価は、この間に相場が急騰したユーロ
と同様に、米トランプ政権発の“ドル不安”の動きによるところが大きいと考えられる。

IMF は 2027 年以降も英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP は増加が続くと予測しているが、その主因は引き続き物
価要因（高インフレ）であり、次いで為替要因（ポンド高）となる。しかしポンドが米国発の流れの中で過大評価さ
れている可能性に鑑みれば、米トランプ政権のレームダック化などでドル不安が和らいだり、あるいは英国を含む
欧州発のショックでポンド安が生じたりすれば、一転して急速なポンド安となる恐れがある。

いずれにしても、コロナショック以降の英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加は高インフレを主因とするものであ
り、英国民の所得の増加を反映したものとは言い難い。ポンド高に関しても、いわゆるドル不安の中で過大評価
が進んだ可能性があるため、何らかのショックで急速なポンド安に転じる懸念も ある。このシナリオが現実のものと
なった場合、英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP は急減することになるだろう。

### Page 3

3. 円安が経済成長につながらなかった日本 
対照的に、コロナショック以降の日本の米ドル建て名目 GDP は減少が続いているが、それは主に為替要因
（円安）によるものである（図表 5）。2023 年以降は日本経済がインフレ基調に転じ、物価要因が押し上げ寄与と
なっているが、一方で成長要因は小幅なプラスにとどまっている。円安は日本経済にとってプラスとの主張 もある
が、実際は円安が経済成長に必ずしもつながっていないことが読み取れる。

他方で、円の対米ドルレート指数（図表 6）は、2021 年頃から実質レートが名目レートを上回るピッチで下落し
ている。日米間の物価上昇の差が著しいためだが、このことは、円が過小評価されている可能性が高いことを意
味している。一方、歴代の日本の政権は、円安による景気浮揚効果を重視してきたが、それが経済成長の加速
につながらなかったことは明らかである。

円相場がわずかに持ち直した 2025 年に成長要因が押し上げ寄与に転じているように、現代の日本の経済構
造は、円安であれば輸出が伸びて成長が加速するといった1990 年代までの構造とは異なる。一方、今年 2 月に
生じたイラン発のエネルギーショックによってインフレ圧力が高まる中でも、円安の是正が進まないばかりか一段
の円安となったことでモノの輸入コストが膨らみ、日本からさらなる所得流出が生じている。

経済成長を伴わない英国の米ドル建て名目 GDP の増加も問題だが、一方で日本の米ドル建て名目 GDP の
減少も大きな問題である。健全な経済成長の下で米ドル建て名目 GDP を増加させていくためには、英国も日本
もまた生産性の向上に代表される経済の体質改善を進める必要がある。特に日本の場合、歴代の政権が円の実
質的な切り下げに活路を見出してきたが、それが経済成長の加速につながらなかった。

今回の日英の名目 GDP の逆転をきっかけに、日本は円と経済成長のあり方に対して、これまでの考え方を冷
静に見つめ直すべきではないだろうか。

超强偏差，超出所有机构预测区间上限。失业率意外从 4.2% 回落⾄ 4.1% ，市场预期维持不
变；平均时薪同⽐增速也加速⾄ 3.1% 。同时上修前两个⽉⾮农数据合计 8.4 万⼈。数据公

息的押注骤然升温。

差。

### Page 2

数据公布后，美国国债遭到抛售， 2 年期美债收益率⼀度上涨 7 个基点⾄ 4.54% ， 10 年期收益率
升⾄ 4.87% 。利率期货市场随即将 12 ⽉加息概率定价⾄ 70% ，市场预计⾄ 2027 年 9 ⽉美联储⾄少
将累计再收紧 40 个基点。
美联储主席沃什上周曾表⽰就业形势 " 相当正常 " ，本次数据料将进⼀步强化其聚焦通胀的政策⽴

就业全⾯⾛强，前两⽉数据⼤幅上修
10 ⽉就业增量 16.5 万，为今年以来第三⾼⽉度读数，且此前数据遭到⼤幅上修，进⼀步凸显劳
动⼒市场的强劲程度。
具体来看，8 ⽉⾮农就业⼈数由 +13.3 万上修⾄ +18.6 万，净上修 5.3 万； 9 ⽉则由 +2.9 万上修⾄
+6.0 万，净上修 3.1 万。两个⽉合计上修 8.4 万⼈，意味着此前市场对劳动⼒市场降温的判断存在
明显低估。
从⾏业分布来看，本次就业增⻓具有较强的⼴度。
医疗健康与社会援助领域新增约 5.5 万⼈，为六个⽉最⾼，该⾏业近年来持续担当就业增⻓的主
要引擎；专业和商业服务、制造业亦录得明显增⻓，整体私⼈部⻔就业增速为今年 3 ⽉以来最

强。

### Page 3

AI 浪潮下就业结构出现分化
在⼈⼯智能快速渗透的背景下，劳动⼒市场内部结构呈现明显分化。
数据中⼼建设需求及 AI 相关基础设施投资，持续⽀撑建筑和制造业就业，⾮住宅建筑领域就业延
续增⻓态势，抵消了住宅建筑领域的拖累。在制造业内部，计算机及电⼦产品制造业就业增幅
为近四年最⾼。
与此同时，信息和⾦融活动等 AI 采⽤率较⾼的⾏业，就业表明出现初步企稳迹象。
⾼盛经济学家本周在研报中指出， "AI 对劳动⼒市场的冲击在近⼏个⽉已愈发清晰可⻅ " ，在⾮制
造业领域， AI 采⽤率越⾼的⾏业，就业增⻓越疲弱，失业率也相应偏⾼。
薪资增速加快，但实际购买⼒仍受压制
薪资数据同样呈现超预期态势，但⼯资增⻓能否跑赢通胀仍是悬⽽未决的问题。
10 ⽉平均时薪环⽐上涨 0.4% ，⾼于市场预期的 0.3% ，为 2025 年 11 ⽉以来最⾼⽉度涨幅；同⽐
增速从上⽉修正后的 3.2% ⼩幅回落⾄ 3.1% ，但⾼于市场此前预期的 3.0% 以下。
值得注意的是，劳动参与率升⾄ 61.9% ，为今年 3 ⽉以来最⾼；但⼴义失业率有所上升，因经济

原因被迫从事兼职⼯作的⼈数升⾄六个⽉⾼点。
此外，失业持续时间的中位数攀升⾄ 11.9 周，为 2021 年以来最⻓，显⽰部分寻职者仍⾯临较⼤
的就业摩擦。⼯资虽在名义上加速增⻓，但在通胀持续⾼企的背景下，实际购买⼒未能同步提
升，这也是本次中期选举期间选⺠情绪的重要写照。
美债承压， 12 ⽉加息预期重燃
强劲就业数据直接冲击固定收益市场，并重塑了市场对美联储政策路径的预期。
数据公布后，美国国债价格全线下跌。对利率路径更为敏感的 2 年期美债收益率升⾄ 4.54% ， 10
年期收益率升⾄ 4.87% 。
利率掉期市场显⽰，交易员⽬前押注 12 ⽉会议加息概率升⾄ 70% ，并预计 2027 年 9 ⽉前美联储将
⾄少再累计加息 40 个基点。
美联储官员在 12 ⽉会议前还将看到⼀份⾮农就业报告及⼀系列通胀数据。
